# First-release requirements and development steps

## Confirmed requirements

| Area | Decision |
| --- | --- |
| Working project name | `jong-bangla-board` |
| Display name | Proposed: Jong Bangla |
| License | MIT |
| Implementation | From scratch, with Rust engine and composition logic |
| First integration | Fcitx5 on Linux |
| Display session | Wayland first |
| First test environment | CachyOS + KDE Plasma; versions to record during setup |
| Phonetic behavior | Target jsAvroPhonetic v1.1.4 conversion behavior; exact revision below |
| Composition | Keep the current word editable until a boundary commits it |
| Backspace | Remove the last raw phonetic keystroke and recompute Bangla |
| Ctrl+Backspace | Clear the active raw buffer without committing; otherwise let the application delete a word |
| Space and Enter | Commit active Bangla, then forward the same key to the application |
| Punctuation | Keep punctuation and phonetic escapes editable until a boundary |
| Escape | Commit active Bangla, clear the raw buffer and consume; otherwise forward |
| Navigation and application shortcuts | Commit active composition, then forward the original event |
| Focus loss and input-method switching | Finalize to the original context when permitted, then clear; discard on explicit reset or destroyed context |
| Choices | Deferred beyond v1; converted Bangla preedit without a Bangla/Latin choice list |
| Numbers | Bangla digits by default |
| Autocorrection | Separate, extensible functionality; no correction rules enabled initially |
| Performance | Fast, lightweight, offline; measurable budgets still to establish |
| Later platforms | IBus and Windows, reusing the Rust engine |

On 2026-10-29, the user deferred the Bangla/Latin choices beyond the first
release. V1 shows converted Bangla in editable preedit and commits that output,
without a choice list or raw Latin selection. Raw keystrokes remain internal
editing state for Backspace. Whether to add choices later remains open.

## Composition model

The engine stores the raw keystrokes for the active word. It derives the Bangla
output from that buffer and exposes the converted preedit to the platform adapter.

**Preedit** is the current, uncommitted text shown in the application. **Commit**
means inserting the converted Bangla output into the application's document.
Before commit, Backspace edits the raw input and recomputes the whole active word.
After commit, ordinary document editing belongs to the application; v1 does not
promise to recover raw keystrokes from already committed Bangla.

**Confirmed on 2026-10-29:** Ctrl+Backspace discards the entire active raw
buffer and consumes the key. With no active input, it reaches the application
for normal word deletion. This includes an active buffer whose converted output
is empty, such as an escape: activity is determined by raw input, not visible text.
A repeated Ctrl+Backspace after clearing the buffer can therefore reach the app.

Space and Enter commit the converted output, then forward the same key exactly
once; Enter can insert a newline or send a message according to the application.
This replaces the earlier proposal to consume the first Enter. Punctuation stays
in the raw buffer until Space, Enter, or another explicit commit. This keeps
multi-key punctuation and escapes editable: periods can become an ellipsis,
and a backtick can change the preceding symbol. Ctrl+Backspace clears all such
pending input together; it does not attempt application-style punctuation-based
word segmentation inside the buffer.

## Composition controller API

Implemented on 2026-10-29 in [composition.rs](../src/composition.rs), with
independent regression sequences in [tests/composition.rs](../tests/composition.rs).
Converted expectations use the already reference-checked rule behavior; editing
and key-forwarding expectations come from this project's user-confirmed contract,
not from the upstream parser.
The public `Composition` type owns a raw UTF-8 buffer and its converted preedit;
it has no candidate selection, document history, desktop events or global state.
It recomputes with the existing `transliterate` function after each edit.

| Operation | State/output contract |
| --- | --- |
| `new()` / `default()` | Empty, inactive controller |
| `is_active()` | True when raw input exists, even if preedit is empty |
| `preedit()` | Borrow the converted output; no raw-input accessor |
| `insert(char)` | Append one non-whitespace, non-control Unicode scalar and recompute; return true |
| Rejected `insert(char)` | Return false without changing state; adapter handles boundaries/shortcuts separately |
| `backspace()` | Pop one raw Unicode scalar, recompute, and return true; false only when inactive |
| `delete_word()` | Discard the whole active buffer without a commit; return whether raw input existed |
| `commit()` | Clear state and return `Some(converted output)`; `None` only when inactive |
| `cancel()` | Discard state without a commit; return whether raw input existed |
| `reset()` | Unconditionally discard state without a commit |
| `handle_key(CompositionKey)` | Return `KeyEffect { consumed, committed }` using the confirmed key table below |

Each accepted Unicode scalar is one editable input unit; Backspace never removes
a byte of Bangla output. Combining characters are separate raw units. Physical
key translation and multi-character input events belong to the adapter, not this
API. `insert` accepts punctuation, digit keys and unmatched Unicode without
adding boundaries. Whitespace and control characters cannot enter the raw buffer;
a rejected insertion is not permission to forward an event while leaving active
composition behind. The adapter must apply its explicit boundary/shortcut policy.

| Normalized `CompositionKey` | Active raw input | No active input |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `Backspace` | Remove one raw scalar; consume; no commit | Forward; no commit |
| `CtrlBackspace` | Clear the entire raw buffer; consume; no commit | Forward; no commit |
| `Space` | Commit, clear state, then forward Space | Forward Space |
| `Enter` | Commit, clear state, then forward Enter | Forward Enter |

The adapter publishes the resulting preedit, sends any nonempty committed output,
then forwards the original key exactly once if `consumed` is false. It must not
insert a second space or newline itself. `Some("")` is a real finalized composition
with empty output; skip the framework's empty commit but preserve key handling.
For example, raw o-backtick is active with empty preedit: Backspace restores অ,
Ctrl+Backspace consumes deletion, and Enter finalizes it and still reaches the app.

Use one controller per input context. `reset()` provides a cleanup operation;
it does not choose whether lifecycle events should commit first. The adapter
applies the [interaction contract](#adapter-interaction-contract) below.
No raw or committed input is logged or persisted; the
controller and its text-bearing result omit Debug formatting. Desktop coverage
remains bounded to the recorded smoke evidence; core batch timings are recorded
in the [resource measurement](#local-resource-measurement-and-leak-check), with
frontend event latency still unmeasured.

## Adapter interaction contract

**Updated by the user on 2026-10-29:** Escape commits active Bangla, clears the
raw buffer and consumes that press; it must preserve the typed word. This
supersedes the earlier Escape-cancels decision. Navigation, Tab, Delete and
application shortcuts commit first, then forward. Modifier-only presses preserve
composition. Focus loss and input-method switching commit to the original field when the framework still permits it, then
clear state. Explicit resets and destroyed contexts discard pending input.
These decisions settle the earlier interaction proposals. They preserve typed
text before application editing; Escape finishes a word without inserting a
space or newline. Explicit reset/destruction and the separate `cancel()` API
retain their discard semantics.

The experimental adapter implements this specification with synthetic validation
and limited user-reported desktop smoke evidence; the full frontend matrix
remains unverified. It uses the existing Rust controller's
actions; no new `CompositionKey` variants are required.
Raw framework events and modifier classification stay outside the Rust core.

| Event reaching the adapter | Active raw input | No active input |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Text key | `insert(char)` and consume accepted text | Same; start composition |
| Plain Backspace | `handle_key(Backspace)` | Forward |
| Exact Ctrl+Backspace | `handle_key(CtrlBackspace)` | Forward |
| Plain Space or Enter | `handle_key(Space/Enter)`; commit, then forward | Forward |
| Plain Escape | `commit()`; clear preedit, commit nonempty Bangla and consume | Forward |
| Navigation, Tab, Delete, application shortcut or other non-text action | `commit()`; clear preedit, commit nonempty output, then forward | Forward |
| Modifier-only press or key release | No composition action; pass through | Same |

Activity always follows raw input, including invisible escapes. After active
Escape commits once, a second Escape reaches the app; after a navigation boundary, subsequent
Backspace belongs to the app. There is no cursor or selection inside raw input
in v1: arrows, Home/End, Page Up/Down, Insert and their selection/word-navigation
variants finalize the whole word before application editing. Tab and Shift+Tab
likewise finalize before completion or moving to another field. Ctrl+C, Ctrl+V,
Ctrl+X, Ctrl+A, Ctrl+Z and other application actions finalize first; the app then
decides what the unchanged shortcut does, including selection and undo behavior.

### Event normalization and ordering

The following engineering rules make the confirmed behavior precise. They avoid
treating shortcut keys as typed text or replaying an event twice.

1. Let Fcitx5 handle its configured activation/switching shortcuts. The adapter
   adds no global language shortcut. If a switch causes deactivation, apply the
   lifecycle contract below, rather than forwarding a second switching key.
2. Classify releases and modifier-only presses before text or boundaries. They
   never insert, delete, commit or cancel. A release may pass through without
   replaying its press; release handling needs actual frontend validation.
3. Match editing keys using the effective symbol and semantic modifiers.
   Ignore lock/repeat bookkeeping when deciding an exact modifier combination;
   preserve the original event for forwarding. Exact Ctrl+Backspace means Ctrl
   without Shift, Alt, Super or other semantic modifiers. Plain Backspace,
   Space, Enter and Escape have none. Keypad Enter follows plain Enter.
4. Shift and Caps Lock affect resolved text, preserving phonetic case. Plain
   digits, keypad digits resolved as text, punctuation and backticks are text.
   Shift+Space/Enter/Backspace/Escape and Ctrl+Shift+Backspace are other actions:
   commit first and forward with their modifiers intact. Shift+Tab is navigation.
5. Ctrl/Alt/Super shortcuts cannot become raw input. Accept a layout-resolved
   AltGr character only when Fcitx5 identifies it as text, with no remaining
   shortcut modifiers. Do not globally erase Ctrl+Alt to make text; ambiguous
   events follow the non-text boundary route until the frontend is verified.
   Dead/compose prefixes with no completed character keep composition unchanged
   and remain available to the framework; resolved text follows the text route.
6. Each delivered repeat press runs the same policy once. Repeated text inserts
   repeatedly; held Backspace consumes only while raw input exists. Repeated
   Escape or Ctrl+Backspace can reach the app after the first press clears input.
   Do not synthesize a second repeat, space or newline.
7. For a handled edit, publish the resulting preedit first, send any nonempty
   commit next, and only then allow the original boundary/action to reach the
   app once. Forwarding means one delivery path: let the framework deliver the
   event or explicitly forward it, never both. Empty-output composition still
   finalizes, without a framework empty commit. Rejected whitespace/control text
   uses the non-text boundary route; rejection alone must not leave stale state.

### Context lifecycle and commit ownership

Keep one controller per input context; never transfer a buffer to another field
or restore it on refocus. On ordinary focus loss or input-method deactivation,
finalize to the original context when its frontend still permits delivery.
If the framework/frontend has already committed the displayed preedit, clear
the Rust buffer without committing again. Otherwise take `commit()` once,
clear displayed preedit, and deliver nonempty output only to that original
context while delivery remains valid. If delivery is unavailable, discard state;
do not retry against a newly focused field. Duplicate callbacks see empty state.

An explicit client reset or context destruction discards state without a commit.
Activation/focus-in begins empty. Framework restrictions on input-method use,
including password-field restrictions, take precedence over key processing;
disabling the input method clears pending state without sending it into the
restricted field. A sensitive-input hint alone is not permission to log text,
persist it, or introduce a new Latin/Bangla mode. No such mode is added here.

Mouse pointer motion alone does not end composition. For an actual preedit click
or a notified cursor/selection relocation, finalize before relocation only when
the original insertion position is still valid. A reset or notification received
after relocation clears state without inserting the old word at the new position.
Do not infer document movement from a candidate-window/caret rectangle change or
ordinary surrounding-text updates. Generic mouse/selection notifications may be
unavailable; the feasibility step must establish what each frontend reports.

Fcitx5's upstream [engine callbacks](https://github.com/fcitx/fcitx5/blob/master/src/lib/fcitx/inputmethodengine.h)
distinguish deactivation from reset. Its [event definitions](https://github.com/fcitx/fcitx5/blob/master/src/lib/fcitx/event.h)
warn that focus-out may follow an already completed preedit commit and expose
key-release and preedit-click events. The [input-context API](https://github.com/fcitx/fcitx5/blob/master/src/lib/fcitx/inputcontext.h)
also distinguishes a context from a guaranteed individual text field. Consulted
on 2026-10-29; these are API investigation evidence, not a validated version or
a guarantee about toolkit event ordering. No upstream source was imported.

### Adapter acceptance cases

These 15 groups define the adapter acceptance scope. The local dispatcher harness
now exercises their synthetic routes, with the limits recorded in the
[feasibility result](#fcitx5-local-feasibility-result). Real frontend/desktop
acceptance is incomplete; limited user observations are recorded below. Core tests also cover raw editing, cancel/reset,
independent contexts and the four normalized keys. The harness records ordered
preedit/commit/application-key effects using only synthetic text such as
`ki` → কি or `o` plus a backtick → empty output, comparing exact strings and counts.

| Case | Required observation |
| --- | --- |
| Escape with `ki`, invisible input, then idle Escape | Commit কি and consume once; invisible input skips an empty commit; idle press forwards |
| Arrow/Home/End/Page/Delete/Insert, including Shift/Ctrl variants | Clear preedit, commit কি once, forward unchanged action once |
| Tab, Shift+Tab and Ctrl+C/V/X/A/Z | Commit before the app action; next text starts fresh |
| Ctrl+Backspace versus Ctrl+Shift+Backspace | Exact Ctrl deletes pending input; extra Shift commits and forwards |
| Space/Enter, keypad Enter and modified variants | One commit then one unchanged key; no added space/newline |
| Shift/Ctrl/Alt/Super press and release without another key | Preserve pending word; no commit or inserted modifier |
| Text and editing-key releases, with active and idle buffers | No second insertion, edit, commit or boundary action |
| Held text/Backspace/Ctrl+Backspace/Escape/Enter | One action per delivered press; empty-state behavior re-evaluated |
| Case-sensitive text, Caps/Num Lock, AltGr, dead/compose sequences | Correct resolved text; no shortcut modifier stripped into text |
| Invisible pending input plus any commit boundary | Clear state, skip empty framework commit, forward once |
| Focus loss or switch with addon-owned commit | Commit once to original context if valid; all local state empty |
| Focus loss with frontend-owned commit, followed by reset/deactivate | No duplicate commit; all local state empty |
| Reset, destruction, unavailable commit target or restricted-field transition | Discard; no stale commit into another field |
| Focus A → B → A and duplicate lifecycle callbacks | No cross-context text or restored raw buffer; next `i` is ই |
| Pointer motion, preedit click, relocation before/after reset | Motion preserves; valid pre-relocation commit or post-relocation discard |

### Fcitx5 local feasibility result

**Implemented and checked on 2026-10-29:** a build-local C++ shared-library addon
and a separate Rust static-library C ABI. This settles the thin C++ bridge as the
practical boundary for this experiment; it does not make C++ a requirement for
future platforms. The core's rules and composition implementation are unchanged.
[Build/check commands](../README.md#build-and-check-the-fcitx5-experiment) and the
[private C header](../adapters/fcitx5/jong.h) make the experiment reproducible.

The addon handles framework key classification, per-context ownership and Fcitx5
preedit/UI/commit calls. The boundary crate only wraps existing Rust controller
actions. Each input-context property uniquely owns one opaque Rust handle;
properties never copy raw composition between fields. Destruction frees it
without committing. No installed addon, desktop process or settings were changed.

**Interoperability contract:**

- Rust allocates/frees the handle; C++ never constructs or deletes its contents.
  Calls use exclusive, non-reentrant access on Fcitx's event thread. Null handles
  are accepted only as an error/no-op; fabricated or freed pointers are invalid.
- Insertion passes a Unicode scalar (`uint32_t`), rejecting surrogates/out-of-range
  values. Rust returns UTF-8 pointer/byte-length spans, without a NUL terminator.
  They are borrowed until the next action/free; C++ copies both spans before
  calling back into Fcitx. Empty spans are never dereferenced. Preedit cursor
  offsets use UTF-8 bytes, including six bytes for synthetic `কি`.
- Status 0 means success; 1 means invalid argument without mutation; 2 means a
  caught Rust panic with state discarded. The separate crate contains the unsafe
  pointer operations; `jong-bangla-core` continues to forbid unsafe code. Rust
  unwinding is caught; panic payloads are forgotten to avoid a second panic from
  their destructor. OOM/abort, invalid pointers and a panic in the runtime hook
  cannot be recovered. Do not build this boundary with `panic=abort` if unwinding
  containment is required. The normal Cargo profiles use unwinding.
- C++ catches exceptions in engine callbacks, discards state and clears preedit
  where possible. An internal key-processing failure consumes that event rather
  than replaying a possibly applied edit. The factory returns null on failure.
  Neither boundary nor addon logs or persists typed text. Standard runtime panic
  diagnostics are not replaced with a process-global panic hook.

**Key and lifecycle evidence:** the actual shared addon is loaded through Fcitx5
5.1.23's addon loader into an isolated `Instance`. Its real event dispatcher routes
synthetic input-context calls to the addon. Ten suites cover the 15 acceptance
groups' synthetic portions: clear-preedit → commit → one application-key delivery;
editing/Escape commit/repeats/releases; resolved case/keypad text; ambiguous AltGr as a
boundary; pending dead/compose prefixes; invisible input; input-method switching;
focus/reset/repeated callbacks; restricted fields; destruction; independent
contexts; fallback preedit without choices; and explicit preedit clicks.

On ordinary focus-out, the framework owns delivery: Fcitx5 commits client preedit
in its `ReservedFirst` handler unless `ClientUnfocusCommit` delegates that to the
frontend. The engine's later `deactivate` only discards local state. The harness
checks the server path and simulates an advertised frontend commit, proving no
extra addon commit. Missing frontend delivery is never retried in another field.
Ordinary input-method switching while focused instead takes the Rust commit once.
Capability-driven switching discards; framework password/disable policy wins.
Fcitx5's configured shortcuts run first: a configured Ctrl+Space switches without
an extra forwarded key, and its modifier-only Shift toggle commits on release.
Keys that reach the addon retain the specified modifier-only policy.

Inspected matching installed headers and upstream tagged sources:
[Fcitx5 5.1.23 dispatcher](https://github.com/fcitx/fcitx5/blob/5.1.23/src/lib/fcitx/instance.cpp),
[input-context delivery](https://github.com/fcitx/fcitx5/blob/5.1.23/src/lib/fcitx/inputcontext.cpp),
[shared-addon loader](https://github.com/fcitx/fcitx5/blob/5.1.23/src/lib/fcitx/sharedlibraryloader.cpp),
and [default shortcuts](https://github.com/fcitx/fcitx5/blob/5.1.23/src/lib/fcitx/globalconfig.cpp).
These informed API use; no upstream implementation or rule tables were copied.
Project sources remain MIT; installed Fcitx5 Core/Config/Utils are linked dynamically
and their upstream headers identify LGPL-2.1-or-later licensing. Distribution and
packaging remain a separate step.

Fcitx5 development headers 5.1.23+; these are build constraints, not a tested
version range. Installed toolkit versions do not establish that any app used them.

**User-reported desktop smoke observations (2026-10-29, clarified):** the user
launched GTK3, Firefox, KWrite and Kate with the private-session instructions.
Ordinary typing and field switching behaved well, with no previous raw buffer
restored. GTK3 and Firefox behaved as expected in the user's tests. In KWrite/
Kate, the user reports Bangla word-editing issues. Their detailed KWrite check
confirms Ctrl+Backspace clears active raw input correctly, but Ctrl+Left/Right do
not perform the expected word movement while Bangla is pending. Ordinary English
word deletion/navigation works. For committed `আমার`, the user reports that three
Ctrl+Backspace presses remove `র`, then `া`, then `আম`, instead of removing the word
at once. They also observe similar behavior with OpenBangla Keyboard.

**User decision for v1:** defer investigation/fixes for KWrite/Kate's Bangla
word-navigation and committed-word deletion behavior; treat this as a documented,
non-blocking application/frontend limitation for the first release. It is not
fixed, and its cause is not established. Keep the confirmed adapter contract:
Ctrl+Backspace clears active raw input, otherwise the application handles word
deletion; navigation commits and forwards the original event. Do not introduce
application-specific shortcut handling or change Bangla word segmentation on
this evidence alone. The OpenBangla comparison is a user observation, not proof
of the responsible layer.

These are limited user observations, not an independently reproduced checklist
or a general desktop support claim. The initial report did not include actual
protocol/runtime fields or full checklist results; later runtime captures are
recorded below. The initial report described the earlier
Escape-cancels build. In a subsequent manual retest, the user confirmed that the
updated Escape-commits behavior works. This confirms the requested interaction;
a detailed per-application Escape edge-case checklist was not supplied.

**Prior KWrite investigation (now deferred for v1):** the inspected KTextEditor 6.31.0
[editing actions](https://github.com/KDE/ktexteditor/blob/v6.31.0/src/view/kateview.cpp)
use application shortcut actions for word movement/deletion. The matching Fcitx
Qt 5.1.16 [Qt6 filter](https://github.com/fcitx/fcitx5-qt/blob/5.1.16/qt6/platforminputcontext/qfcitxplatforminputcontext.cpp)
shares its implementation with
[the Qt5 filter](https://github.com/fcitx/fcitx5-qt/blob/5.1.16/qt5/platforminputcontext/qfcitxplatforminputcontext.cpp),
which normally returns unhandled keys through Qt's window-system event path.
This identifies relevant routing layers; it does not establish the cause of the
reported failures. No Qt shortcut workaround or modifier reinterpretation has
been added. Installed packages currently report Kate 26.08.2-1.1, KTextEditor
6.31.0-1.1, Fcitx5 Qt 5.1.16-2.1, Qt6 base 6.12.0-3.1 and GTK3 1:3.24.52-2.1;
that inventory alone does not verify the exact runtime paths used by the tester.

**Remaining validation:** the harness frontend is synthetic, neither Wayland nor
XWayland. It does not complete a real AltGr/dead-key/compose pipeline, perform
application mouse/selection relocation, prove an actual frontend commit, or render
fallback UI. Cursor rectangles/surrounding-text notifications preserve composition;
a notified preedit click finalizes, and reset-before-click discards. Generic mouse
motion and document selection may not be reported. No desktop support, latency,
memory budget or full compatibility claim follows from these checks.

**Remaining frontend scope:** the later runtime captures and overall app passes
below complete the bounded smoke evidence handoff with explicit limits. Full
frontend acceptance would additionally need per-case observations, uncovered
routes and original-field focus-out delivery evidence. Escape is user-confirmed;
KWrite/Kate word editing stays deferred for v1. The later bounded core timings
and leak checks are recorded in the [resource measurement](#local-resource-measurement-and-leak-check).
Packaging and installation guidance remain separate increments. Keep system
input-method settings unchanged until that setup is explicitly in scope.

A temporary manual-test helper was prepared outside the repository. Its isolated
D-Bus/Fcitx startup, Jong metadata discovery and test-shell exit were checked;
its GTK source was syntax-checked. No GUI interactions were tested by the agent.
The helper creates temporary settings and disposable GTK3/browser fields, keeps
the existing desktop input method untouched, and disables key tracing. Requested
test routes use native Wayland windows with explicit Fcitx GTK/Qt input modules
over the private D-Bus session. They do not establish KWin text-input protocol
integration or XWayland support. Confirm app startup and actual runtime details
during the manual tests; the temporary helper is not installation tooling.

**Manual follow-up and helper correction (2026-10-30):** the user reports that
`browser-test` (Firefox) and `qt-test` (KWrite) passed with no issues. Retain that
as an overall user-reported pass; detailed per-case observations were not
supplied. Runtime evidence from the subsequent attachments is recorded below.
Do not infer coverage for optional or
unavailable routes, or reverse the accepted KWrite/Kate word-editing deferral.

The new GTK3 test did not start: the helper requested Gtk 3.0 but imported Gdk
without selecting its version. PyGI selected Gdk 4.0 first, conflicting with
Gtk 3.0's Gdk 3.0 dependency. The exact import failure was reproduced locally.
Added `gi.require_version('Gdk', '3.0')` before imports in both the active
temporary `gtk-test.py` and the launcher template. Fresh subprocess imports now
select Gtk/Gdk 3.0 and GTK runtime 3.24.52 with no warnings; shell and Python
syntax checks pass. This fixes the test helper; no engine or adapter code changed.
Syntax-only validation in the earlier step did not detect the dependency conflict.
After the correction, the user confirmed that GTK is working fine. All three
private-session test apps now have an overall user-reported pass. This closes
the failed GTK startup/retest; no detailed per-case results or runtime/backend
line were supplied with that confirmation. Subsequent console/about:support
attachments provide the runtime evidence below. No further GTK rerun is required
solely for the corrected import; optional-route coverage remains unrecorded.

| --- | --- | --- |
| Firefox | `application.name=Firefox`, `version=159.0`, `buildID=20261027211755`, `distributionID=archlinux`; OS reports Linux 7.3.0-4-cachyos | `graphics.windowProtocol=wayland`; native Wayland is directly reported. No GTK runtime version is present in the supplied library-version fields. |
| GTK3 helper | Console prints `GTK runtime: 3 24 52 display backend: GdkWaylandDisplay` | GTK 3.24.52 and native Wayland directly reported after the import fix. |
| KWrite | Console identifies `kwrite` opening the disposable `qt-test.txt`; no application/Qt runtime versions printed | Launcher sets `QT_QPA_PLATFORM=wayland` and `QT_IM_MODULE=fcitx`; this is the requested route, without an independent runtime backend report. Prior package inventory is `kate` 26.08.2-1.1 / `qt6-base` 6.12.0-3.1, not a runtime-version observation. |

Firefox's supplied environment also reports `MOZ_ENABLE_WAYLAND=1` and
`GDK_BACKEND=wayland`, consistent with its runtime protocol field. The private
launcher's explicit IM modules use its separate Fcitx D-Bus session; native
Wayland windows alone do not validate KWin text-input protocol integration.

**Console warnings:** service activation for `org.freedesktop.systemd1` fails
on the private bus; GTK/portal processes report AT-SPI/atk-bridge warnings.
Portal output reports a GTK fallback, missing RealtimeKit properties and app-ID
registration failures; KWallet reports a missing socket. Qt reports missing
OpenType support in multiple candidate fonts and queued notifications at teardown.
Firefox reports a broken pipe while writing selection data. These messages name
desktop-service, accessibility, font and selection paths; they do not report a
Jong panic or addon crash. Their root causes were not reproduced or fixed in this
evidence-only step. Retain the user's successful typing report without inferring
that accessibility, portals, clipboard/selection delivery or every font path was
validated. Any visible failure in those areas would need its own reproducer.

**Bounded smoke result:** all three apps have user-reported functional passes,
with direct GTK/Firefox Wayland evidence and the Qt limitations stated above.
Detailed per-case results, AltGr/dead-key/Compose, Electron, XWayland, KWin
text-input protocols and exact frontend commit ownership are untested or
unrecorded; this is not full desktop acceptance or a general support claim.
Missing KWrite runtime fields stay unrecorded rather than blocking this evidence
handoff. The KWrite/Kate word-editing deferral is unchanged. Further input tests
should target a named uncovered route or a reproducible new failure; no routine
repeat of these three app tests is required.

### Remaining desktop smoke checklist

Use disposable text in GTK3 and Firefox fields, then a disposable KWrite document.
Reuse the earlier basic typing and Escape evidence. First check `ki` then Space
produces `কি ` to confirm that each new test window reaches Jong. Start each case
in an empty field/document or a separate disposable line. Here, *pending* means
the underlined word still being edited before Space or Enter commits it.

| Case | Procedure and observation to report |
| --- | --- |
| Raw editing | Type `ki`, Backspace: ক; type `i`: কি. Ctrl+Backspace clears the pending word; a fresh `i` produces ই. |
| Field focus and return | In GTK3/Firefox, type pending `ki` in A, Tab to B and type `i`. A should contain কি once, B ই. Return to A and check that its old pending buffer does not resume. |
| Window focus | With pending `ki`, switch to another window and return. Report whether কি stays once in the original field and whether the next `i` starts fresh; no old text should appear in the other window. |
| Input-method switch | With pending `ki`, Ctrl+Space to English, type `x`, then Ctrl+Space back and type `i`. Report one committed কি, literal `x`, then fresh ই; no duplicate or restored raw buffer. |
| Modifiers, case and repeats | With pending `ki`, press/release Shift and Ctrl alone, then continue typing. Report preservation or a focus/shortcut action. On fresh input compare `ki` (কি) with `kI` (কী); hold a letter and Backspace briefly, checking for stuck keys, duplication on release or freezing. |
| Enter and application shortcuts | In a multiline field, `ki` then Enter should insert কি and one newline. With pending `ki`, Ctrl+A should commit before selecting; typing `i` should replace the selection with ই. Report extra or missing actions. |
| Pointer and cursor relocation | Move the pointer over pending `ki`, then continue: motion alone should preserve it. Separately click elsewhere in the document or select text while input is pending. Report where কি ends up and whether the next `i` begins fresh. A reset may discard pending text; stale text must not be inserted at the new cursor. |
| Restricted field / context closure | In GTK3/Firefox, move from pending `ki` to the dummy password field, then back. Report any original-field commit/discard, password-field composition or stale buffer. Close a test window with pending input and open a new one; its first `i` should be fresh ই. Never use a real password. |
| AltGr / dead key / Compose | Use only an already configured layout/Compose key. Report the exact sequence, expected character, actual result and whether a word was pending. Otherwise mark unavailable; do not change system settings for this test. |

For each application, record its version, toolkit version where exposed and
Wayland/XWayland path, distinguishing an observed runtime value from a forced
launch setting or installed package version. The GTK helper prints its runtime
and display backend. Firefox's `about:support` exposes its version and Window
Protocol; report only those fields. KWrite's Help/About dialogs expose application
and Qt versions where available. Record unavailable fields explicitly.

Report `pass`, `fail` or `unavailable` per case and app. For a failure include
the field, exact keys, expected and actual text, whether the word was pending,
and whether it repeats on a second attempt. A screenshot or short recording is
optional when cursor/underline behavior is hard to describe. Keep Escape and
KWrite/Kate word navigation/committed-word deletion outside this retest. Direct
reset/destruction callbacks, unavailable commit targets and frontend commit
ownership still have synthetic evidence only where no desktop observation is
available; do not infer them from a passing visible interaction.

## Proposed architecture

1. **Transliteration core (Rust):** deterministic raw-input-to-Bangla conversion;
   no knowledge of Fcitx5, Wayland, dictionaries, or desktop UI.
2. **Composition controller (Rust, implemented):** raw buffer, converted Bangla
   preedit, editing, and explicit commit/cancel/reset actions.
3. **Fcitx5 adapter (local experiment implemented):** translates framework events
   into controller actions and sends preedit/committed text to Fcitx5. Desktop
   validation and packaging remain separate.

**Implemented internal organization (2026-10-29):** the existing Rust crate now
keeps `src/lib.rs` as the public entry point, re-exporting the unchanged
`transliterate(&str) -> String` API. Private `transliteration` modules separate
the scanner and per-call context, authored rule tables, ordinary vowel handling,
and following-`r` handling. Cross-family matching order stays explicit in the
scanner because consumed keys cannot start later rules. Higher-priority `rri`,
ng-vowel, and `aZ`/`oZ` branches remain there. That organizational refactor added
no phonetic coverage, dependencies, composition or adapters. The subsequent
composition step implements the independent controller above; the
[local feasibility step](#fcitx5-local-feasibility-result) adds its separate adapter.

Autocorrection should be a separate optional stage that can be added without
changing the deterministic core. The first implementation need not include a
plugin loader, a dictionary, or a no-op extension framework.

The official [Fcitx5 addon tutorial](https://fcitx-im.org/wiki/Develop_an_simple_input_method)
documents C++ shared-library addons and CMake. Investigate the smallest practical
Rust interoperability approach with a minimal addon experiment. The completed
local experiment uses a thin C++ addon and a Rust C ABI; its boundary and build
instructions are recorded above. An entirely Rust addon has not been investigated.

The official [Fcitx5 Wayland guide](https://fcitx-im.org/wiki/Using_Fcitx_5_on_Wayland/en)
describes compositor- and application-dependent integration. Test on CachyOS +
KDE Plasma first and record versions. An XWayland application test is useful but
does not establish native Wayland support. Broader support requires more testing.

## Phonetic behavior reference

Selected on 2026-10-28: **jsAvroPhonetic v1.1.4**, from
[torifat/jsAvroPhonetic](https://github.com/torifat/jsAvroPhonetic), pinned to
commit [`d3790172ebff92d43f0294bc51fed1fca7c80039`](https://github.com/torifat/jsAvroPhonetic/tree/d3790172ebff92d43f0294bc51fed1fca7c80039).

[OmicronLab's developer resources](https://www.omicronlab.com/developers.html)
identify this project as its official JavaScript implementation. It offers a
conversion reference that can be exercised independently of a desktop input
method. `git ls-remote` confirmed v1.1.4 as the newest published tag on the
selection date; the pinned `bower.json` also reports 1.1.4. The repository's
current default branch is a different revision. Pinning the tagged revision
keeps future comparisons repeatable rather than following a moving branch.

The scope is deterministic phonetic conversion to Unicode Bangla. Dictionary
suggestions, autocorrection, candidate ranking, and browser UI behavior are
outside this comparison. Composition controls remain this project's separate
decisions. This reference selection does not establish parity with all Avro
implementations or current Avro Keyboard for Windows.

The [README at the pinned revision](https://github.com/torifat/jsAvroPhonetic/blob/d3790172ebff92d43f0294bc51fed1fca7c80039/README.md)
declares MPL-1.1. It is a behavior reference, not a dependency or a source of
copied code: no upstream engine, rule tables, or fixtures have been imported into
the project.
Our implementation and initial examples are independently authored under
the project's MIT license. Any later import requires its own provenance and
license review.

## Transliteration API

`jong_bangla_core::transliterate(&str) -> String` converts supported phonetic
sequences and ASCII digits. The ASCII capitals `ABCEFHKLMPQVWX` alias their
lowercase forms before matching; all other letters retain case-sensitive behavior.
Matching uses verified priorities; it is not a general longest-match rule across all families.
Consumed keys cannot begin another match. For example, `kkh` wins before `kk`,
but `kk` consumes the first two keys of `kkkh`, giving ক্কখ.

Vowels use context from raw tokens matched within the current call. Copied
Bangla does not supply that context. Unmatched Unicode characters pass through
without normalization; an unsupported spelling may still contain supported
parts that convert. Calls share no state, and the input is never modified or
retained. Stateful editing is provided separately by the
[composition controller](#composition-controller-api); platform integration remains future work.

The [coverage overview](../README.md#current-status) links the detailed rule
sections below. Each records its exact Unicode behavior, context, following-`r`
exceptions, consuming priorities, and independently authored fixture evidence.
Those sections remain the home for detailed compatibility decisions. Older sections
record each increment's then-current limits; the [selective case-alias batch](#selective-ascii-case-alias-batch)
resolves their A/B/C/E/F/H/K/L/M/P/Q/V/W/X and AZ limitations. The
[explicit G/J batch](#explicit-gj-spelling-batch) resolves their G/J limitations,
including exposed G after consumed keys and split-call outputs. The
[q/z/R/Rh following-r batch](#following-r-after-q-z-r-and-rh) resolves their
basic following-r gaps; the symbol and escape batches resolve those standalone
forms. The [reph batch](#reph-batch) supersedes older reph limitations and
partial outputs. Further context coverage and Unicode case handling remain as
described in the current handoff.

**Documentation organization (2026-10-29):** crate documentation keeps a short
overview, limitations, and three executable examples. Function documentation
keeps the API contract. Inline comments explain non-obvious ordering, raw-key
context, Unicode boundaries, and joiners beside the affected code. Exhaustive
examples belong in tests and fixtures rather than a growing source comment
catalog. The 53 removed crate examples are preserved as an integration test;
no transliteration behavior or compatibility scope changes.

## First conversion slice

Implemented on 2026-10-28 as a single Rust library package, `jong-bangla-core`.
`transliterate(&str) -> String` performs conversion without platform APIs, mutable
composition state, external dependencies, or input persistence. Rust 2024 edition
and Rust/Cargo 1.99.1 are the initial development baseline, matching the validated
current stable toolchain. Older toolchains have not been tested.

The initial sequence set is deliberately small; the vowel groups below extend
this baseline:

| Raw input | Output | Unicode |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `k`, `kh` | ক, খ | U+0995, U+0996 |
| `g`, `gh` | গ, ঘ | U+0997, U+0998 |
| `t`, `th` | ত, থ | U+09A4, U+09A5 |
| `T`, `Th` | ট, ঠ | U+099F, U+09A0 |
| `n`, `N` | ন, ণ | U+09A8, U+09A3 |
| `b`, `bh` | ব, ভ | U+09AC, U+09AD |
| `m` | ম | U+09AE |
| `0`–`9` | ০–৯ | U+09E6–U+09EF |

In the initial slice, the longest supported consonant sequence was selected at
each input position, with exact case matching. Otherwise one complete Unicode
character was copied unchanged. The vowel groups add context below.
Unsupported sequences can contain supported parts, so preservation applies to
unmatched characters, not necessarily an entire word. No Unicode normalization
is performed. Existing Bangla, non-ASCII digits, and other unmatched Unicode
characters are preserved. This slice does not provide composition controls;
those require the later controller. The originally planned literal Latin
candidate was deferred beyond v1 on 2026-10-29.

Reference-checked cases cover the 13 consonant sequences individually, all ten
digits individually and together, `kh12T`, `t T th Th n N`, and `[2026]`.
The fixtures were authored for this project; no upstream tests or rule tables
were imported. The external reference parser was downloaded only to a temporary
directory and evaluated for comparison with Node.js v24.21.0. Its SHA-256 is
`312ed3438ebf0a69f6de38036572ec39cdbafe3ef53f500caa402aaa704c2b32`.

Initial gaps observed with the same reference parser (`a` and `ka` are now
resolved by the first vowel group below):

| Input | Initial slice | Reference | Missing behavior |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `a` | a | আ (U+0986) | Independent vowels |
| `ka` | কa | কা (U+0995 U+09BE) | Contextual vowel signs |
| `kkh` | কখ | ক্ষ (U+0995 U+09CD U+09B7) | Conjunct sequences |
| `K` | K | ক (U+0995) | Case normalization for some letters |
| `` k`h `` | ক`h | কহ (U+0995 U+09B9) | Escape handling and unsupported `h` |

These are temporary coverage limits, not intended v1 behavior. Punctuation and
backticks are currently literal; they do not commit text or apply phonetic
rules. Arbitrary concatenations of supported consonants are not a compatibility
claim because longer or contextual rules are still absent. Backspace, commit,
cancel, focus handling, and platform testing remain later work.

## First vowel group

Added on 2026-10-28: lowercase `a` and `i`, with context tracked locally during
each conversion call. This is transliteration context, not editable composition
state, and nothing is retained between calls.

| Context | `a` | `i` |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Start, or after a digit/unmatched character | আ (U+0986) | ই (U+0987) |
| Immediately after a supported consonant token | া (U+09BE) | ি (U+09BF) |
| Immediately after lowercase `i` | য়া (U+09DF U+09BE) | ই (U+0987) |
| Immediately after lowercase `a` | আ (U+0986) | ই (U+0987) |

For example, `ka` now produces `কা`, `ki` produces `কি`, `ami` produces `আমি`,
and `kia` produces `কিয়া`. The vowel sign for `i` is encoded after its consonant
(U+0995 U+09BF in `কি`), even though a font displays the sign to its left. The
`য়` in the `ia` sequence is U+09DF, matching the reference exactly; the engine
does not normalize or substitute a visually similar Unicode sequence.

Only recognized consonant tokens establish consonant context. Existing Bangla,
emoji, punctuation, whitespace, combining marks, digits, and unsupported Latin
letters end that context. Thus `কi` produces `কই`, and `k1a` produces `ক১আ`.
This prevents vowel signs from attaching to copied Unicode text. Vowel context
also ends at such characters: `i a` produces `ই আ`, without an inserted `য়`.

The 66 independently authored [vowel cases](../tests/fixtures/vowels.tsv) cover
each supported consonant with both vowels, repeated and adjacent vowels, digits,
word separators, mixed Unicode, and several words. Their expected output and
code points were checked against the pinned parser before the Rust rules were
implemented. Together with the original 27 cases, all 93 match. The Rust tests
also check control-character boundaries, combining marks, and separate calls.

That increment left uppercase `A`/`I`, other vowels, and context after unsupported
Latin consonants unimplemented. The next group below adds `u`/`U`.
At this step, `qa` produced `qআ` while the reference produces `কা`;
the later [lowercase `q` increment](#basic-lowercase-q-consonant) resolves that gap.
Backtick escapes still pass through literally: `` i` `` produces `ই` followed
by a backtick, while the reference produces U+09BF alone. No broader compatibility,
composition, or platform support is established by this increment.

## Second vowel group

Added on 2026-10-28: case-sensitive `u` and `U`, extending the same conversion-local
context used by `a`/`i`.

| Context | `u` | `U` |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Start, or after a digit/unmatched character | উ (U+0989) | ঊ (U+098A) |
| Immediately after a supported consonant token | ু (U+09C1) | ূ (U+09C2) |
| Immediately after any supported vowel | উ (U+0989) | ঊ (U+098A) |

An `a` immediately after `u` or `U` produces য়া (U+09DF U+09BE), as it already
does after `i`. Thus `kua` → `কুয়া` and `kUa` → `কূয়া`. An `i` after either form
remains independent: `kui` → `কুই`, `kUi` → `কূই`. Repeated vowels are separate:
`uu` → `উউ`, `uU` → `উঊ`, `Uu` → `ঊউ`, `UU` → `ঊঊ`.

Case remains meaningful: `bhumi` → `ভুমি`, `bhUmi` → `ভূমি`. Both results follow
the typed sequence without a dictionary or spelling correction. Other examples
include `tumi` → `তুমি` and `khuki` → `খুকি`.

The 80 new [fixtures](../tests/fixtures/vowels-u.tsv) were independently authored
and reference-checked before the Rust changes. They cover both vowels with all
13 supported consonants, all vowel pairs involving `u`/`U` and existing `a`/`i`,
neighboring-vowel transitions, digits, boundaries, mixed Unicode, and words.
All 173 cases now match the pinned parser. Existing context boundaries and
per-call reset behavior apply to both new vowels; for example, `কU` → `কঊ`,
`k1u` → `ক১উ`, and `u a` → `উ আ`.

That increment left uppercase `A`/`I`, remaining vowels, unsupported consonants,
conjuncts, and phonetic punctuation/escapes unimplemented. The next group below
adds `I`. In particular, `` u` `` remains `উ` followed
by a backtick rather than the reference's standalone U+09C1 sign. The gap probes
at that step were `I`, `kkh`, `K`, `` k`h ``, and `` u` ``.
No dependencies, composition controls, or platform integration were added.

## Third vowel group

Added on 2026-10-28: uppercase `I`, preserving the distinction from lowercase `i`.

| Context | `I` output |
| --- | --- |
| Start, or after a digit/unmatched character or supported vowel | ঈ (U+0988) |
| Immediately after a supported consonant token | ী (U+09C0) |

For example, `ki` → `কি` (U+0995 U+09BF), while `kI` → `কী` (U+0995 U+09C0).
The engine follows the typed case without spelling correction: `nIti` → `নীতি`,
`bhUmI` → `ভূমী`. An `a` immediately after `I` produces য়া (U+09DF U+09BE),
as it already does after `i`/`u`/`U`: `kIa` → `কীয়া`. Other adjacent vowels stay
independent; for example, `Iu` → `ঈউ`, and repeated `II` → `ঈঈ`.

The 52 independently authored [fixtures](../tests/fixtures/vowels-i-long.tsv)
were checked against the pinned parser before the Rust changes. They cover `I`
after all 13 supported consonants, every pair involving `I` and an existing vowel,
longer vowel transitions, digits, separators, mixed Unicode, and words. All 225
cases match the reference. Boundary and separate-call regressions now include
`I`; copied Bangla still ends consonant context (`কI` → `কঈ`).

Uppercase `A`, remaining vowels, unsupported consonants, conjuncts, phonetic
punctuation/escapes, and composition/platform integration remain incomplete.
For example, `` I` `` remains `ঈ` followed by a backtick, while the reference
produces the standalone U+09C0 sign. Gap probes at that step covered `e`, `kkh`,
`K`, `` k`h ``, and `` I` ``; the next group below adds `e`. No broader compatibility
or performance claim is made.

## Fourth vowel group

Added on 2026-10-28: lowercase `e` and the two-key sequence `ee`. The longest
supported sequence is matched first, so `ee` is one long vowel rather than two
independent `e` vowels. It shares the existing uppercase `I` conversion.

| Context | `e` output | `ee` output |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Start, or after a digit/unmatched character or supported vowel | এ (U+098F) | ঈ (U+0988) |
| Immediately after a supported consonant token | ে (U+09C7) | ী (U+09C0) |

For example, `ke` → `কে` (U+0995 U+09C7), and `kee` → `কী` (U+0995 U+09C0).
Longer runs remain greedy: `eee` → `ঈএ`, `eeee` → `ঈঈ`, and `keee` → `কীএ`.
An `a` following either form produces য়া (U+09DF U+09BE): `kea` → `কেয়া`,
`keea` → `কীয়া`. Other adjacent vowels stay independent; `ie` → `ইএ`,
`ei` → `এই`, and `eu` → `এউ`.

The 92 independently authored [fixtures](../tests/fixtures/vowels-e.tsv) were
checked against the pinned parser. They cover `e` and `ee` after all 13 supported
consonants, neighboring vowels, longer runs, digits, boundaries, mixed Unicode,
and words such as `mene` → `মেনে` and `buke` → `বুকে`. All 317 cases match.
Context resets at copied characters and between calls as before: `কe` → `কএ`,
`কee` → `কঈ`, and `e a` → `এ আ`.

Uppercase `A`/`E`, remaining vowels, unsupported consonants, conjuncts, phonetic
punctuation/escapes, and composition/platform integration remain incomplete.
For example, `` e` `` remains `এ` followed by a backtick instead of the reference's
standalone U+09C7 sign. At that increment, `kemon` produced `কেমoন`, with
unsupported `o` preserved; the next group below resolves it. Gap probes at that
step covered `o`, `kkh`, `K`, `` k`h ``, and `` e` ``.
This increment adds no dependencies or broader support/performance claims.

## Fifth vowel group

Added on 2026-10-28: case-sensitive `o`/`O` and greedy `oo`, which aliases short
`u`. Context now retains the last raw vowel key as well as distinguishing vowels
from supported consonants and copied characters. It remains local to one call.

| Context | `o` output | `O` output | `oo` output |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Start, or after a digit/unmatched character | অ (U+0985) | ও (U+0993) | উ (U+0989) |
| Immediately after a supported consonant | No character | ো (U+09CB) | ু (U+09C1) |
| After a supported raw vowel key other than lowercase `o` | ও (U+0993) | ও (U+0993) | উ (U+0989) |
| After a raw lowercase `o`, including the end of an `oo` token | অ (U+0985) | ও (U+0993) | উ (U+0989) |

Greedy matching applies before context: a leading `oo` is one token, not two
single `o` tokens. Examples: `ko` → `ক`, `kO` → `কো`, `koo` → `কু`,
`ooo` → `উঅ`, and `oooo` → `উউ`. The uppercase `O` sign is the single code point
U+09CB, matching the reference without normalization.

After lowercase `o` has added no character, it still establishes vowel context.
Thus `koO` → `কও`, rather than adding a sign to the consonant. The raw key also
matters after aliases: `eeo` → `ঈও` (last raw key `e`), while `ooo` → `উঅ`
(last key of `oo` is `o`). This is why context does not follow the output glyphs
or just the normalized alias (`I`/`u`).

An `a` after any of the new forms produces য়া (U+09DF U+09BE): `koa` → `কয়া`,
`kOa` → `কোয়া`, and `kooa` → `কুয়া`. Context after raw `a` is now retained too,
so `ao` → `আও`; repeated `aa` continues to produce `আআ`. Digits, whitespace,
copied Unicode, and unsupported characters reset context as before.

The 156 independently authored [fixtures](../tests/fixtures/vowels-o.tsv) were
checked against the pinned parser before Rust implementation. They cover all
13 supported consonants with all three forms, neighboring vowels and aliases,
longer runs, boundaries, Unicode, and words. All 473 cases match. Examples now
include `kemon` → `কেমন`, `onek` → `অনেক`, and `tOmake` → `তোমাকে`.

That increment deferred the two-key vowels `OI`/`OU`: it produced `ওঈ`/`ওঊ`, while
the reference produces ঐ (U+0990)/ঔ (U+0994). The next group below resolves this.
Uppercase `A`/`E`, other phonetic sequences, unsupported consonants, conjuncts,
punctuation/escapes, composition, and platform integration remain incomplete.
For example, `` O` `` still produces `ও` followed by a backtick instead of U+09CB.
Gap probes at that step covered `OI`, `kkh`, `K`, `` k`h ``, and `` O` ``. This increment
adds no dependencies or broader compatibility/performance claims.

## Sixth vowel group

Added on 2026-10-28: the case-sensitive two-key vowels `OI` and `OU`. Each pair
matches before single `O` and consumes two ASCII keys at a valid UTF-8 boundary.

| Context | `OI` output | `OU` output |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Start, or after a digit/unmatched character or supported vowel | ঐ (U+0990) | ঔ (U+0994) |
| Immediately after a supported consonant | ৈ (U+09C8) | ৌ (U+09CC) |

For example, `kOI` → `কৈ` (U+0995 U+09C8), `kOU` → `কৌ` (U+0995 U+09CC),
and `nOUka` → `নৌকা`. Both dependent signs are emitted as single code points,
without normalization or substituting visually similar sequences.

The match is exact: `OI` → `ঐ`, but `Oi` → `ওই`, `oI` → `অঈ`, and `oi` → `অই`.
Likewise, `OU` → `ঔ`, while `Ou` → `ওউ`, `oU` → `অঊ`, and `ou` → `অউ`.
Adjacent pairs remain separate: `OIOU` → `ঐঔ` and `OUOI` → `ঔঐ`.

Conversion context retains the pair's final raw key (`I` or `U`), rather than
its initial `O`. Existing following-vowel behavior applies: `OIa` → `ঐয়া`,
`OUa` → `ঔয়া`, `OIo` → `ঐও`, and `OUoo` → `ঔউ`. Copied characters reset context,
and separate calls share no state; `কOI` → `কঐ` and `OU a` → `ঔ আ`.

The 150 independently authored [fixtures](../tests/fixtures/vowels-oi-ou.tsv)
were checked against the pinned parser before Rust implementation. They cover
all 13 supported consonants, existing vowels and aliases before/after each pair,
longer transitions, case variants, digits, boundaries, mixed Unicode, and words.
All 623 reference cases match. No dependencies or platform code were added.

That increment left `rri` (ঋ/ৃ), uppercase `A`/`E`, unsupported consonants,
other phonetic sequences, conjuncts, punctuation/escapes, composition, and platform
integration incomplete. For example, `` OU` `` still produces `ঔ` followed
by a backtick instead of the reference's standalone U+09CC sign. Gap probes at
that step covered `rri`, `kkh`, `K`, `` k`h ``, and `` OU` ``; the next group below
adds `rri`. No broader compatibility
or performance claim is made.

## Seventh vowel group

Added on 2026-10-28: exact lowercase `rri`, matched before individual consonants
and consumed as three ASCII keys at a valid UTF-8 boundary.

| Context | `rri` output |
| --- | --- |
| Start, or after a digit/unmatched character or supported vowel | ঋ (U+098B) |
| Immediately after a supported consonant | ৃ (U+09C3) |

The later [following-`rri` increment](#following-rri-after-ng-ng-and-ng) handles
the matching-order exception after `Ng`/`NG`/`ng`; these were not in this initial set.

For example, `krri` → `কৃ` (U+0995 U+09C3), `krriti` → `কৃতি`, and
`rriN` → `ঋণ`. The sign is emitted as the single code point U+09C3; the engine
does not normalize or substitute visually similar Unicode sequences.

Following-vowel context uses the final raw `i`: `rria` → `ঋয়া`, `rrio` → `ঋও`,
and `krriooa` → `কৃউয়া`. Adjacent patterns remain separate (`rrirri` → `ঋঋ`).
Copied characters and digits reset context: `কrri` → `কঋ`, `k1rri` → `ক১ঋ`,
and `rri a` → `ঋ আ`. Separate calls share no state.

The 84 independently authored [fixtures](../tests/fixtures/vowels-rri.tsv) were
checked against the pinned parser before Rust implementation. They cover all
13 supported consonants, existing vowels and aliases on either side, repetition,
longer transitions, digits, boundaries, copied Unicode, and words. All 707
reference cases match. Principal forms for all 11 basic vowels are implemented;
this does not establish full vowel-context or Avro compatibility.

That increment left standalone `r`/`rr` and their case variants unsupported.
The longer runs interacted with that matching, exposing these initial gaps:

| Input | Initial `rri` slice | Reference |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `rrri` | rঋ | রররি |
| `rrrri` | rrঋ | ররৃ |
| `Rri` | Rrই | ড়্রি |

The next `r`/`rr` group below resolves the overlapping lowercase runs. The `Rri`
case remains incomplete because related conjunct behavior is still missing;
basic uppercase `R` is now implemented in the `R`/`Rh` group below. Uppercase
`A`/`E`, other consonants, conjuncts, punctuation/escapes, composition, and platform integration remain
incomplete. In particular, `` rri` `` still produces `ঋ` followed by a backtick
instead of the reference's standalone U+09C3 sign. Gap probes at that step covered
`r`, `rrri`, `Rri`, `kkh`, and `` rri` ``. No dependencies or broader support/performance
claims were added.

## Basic r and rr consonants

Added on 2026-10-28: lowercase `r` → র (U+09B0) and `rr` → রর
(U+09B0 U+09B0) in the basic matching subset. There are now 15 consonant input
sequences producing 14 distinct consonant letters.

Matching prioritizes `rri` over `rr`, then `r`. This retains `rri` → `ঋ` and
`krri` → `কৃ`, while fixing `rrri` → `রররি` (U+09B0 U+09B0 U+09B0 U+09BF)
and `rrrri` → `ররৃ` (U+09B0 U+09B0 U+09C3). In the latter, the first `rr` is
followed by `rri`, which supplies a dependent vowel sign. `rrI` → `ররী` stays
distinct from `rri` → `ঋ`.

Both consonant patterns establish consonant context. Examples: `ra` → `রা`,
`ri` → `রি`, `ro` → `র`, `rra` → `ররা`, and `rre` → `ররে`. Copied Unicode,
digits, and separators still reset context: `r1a` → `র১আ`, `r🙂a` → `র🙂আ`,
and `rকa` → `রকআ`. No state is shared between calls.

The 115 independently authored [fixtures](../tests/fixtures/consonants-r.tsv)
were checked against the pinned parser before Rust implementation. They cover
both patterns, supported vowels/aliases, overlapping `rri` runs, basic `r`
before supported consonants, boundaries, Unicode, and words such as `ram` → `রাম`,
`rani` → `রানি`, and `kora` → `করা`. All 822 reference cases match.

This increment does not implement the reference's context-sensitive conjunct/reph
forms. Examples at that step (`kr` is now covered by the
[basic following-`r` batch](#following-r-after-basic-k-kh-and-sh)):

| Input | Engine at that step | Reference |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `kr` | কর | ক্র (U+0995 U+09CD U+09B0) |
| `rrk` | ররক | র্ক (U+09B0 U+09CD U+0995) |
| `rrr` | ররর | র্র (U+09B0 U+09CD U+09B0) |
| `Rri` | ড়রি | ড়্রি (U+09DC U+09CD U+09B0 U+09BF) |

These are explicit coverage limits, not intended v1 behavior. Other remaining
consonants, uppercase aliases, punctuation/escapes, composition, and platform
integration also remain incomplete. Gap probes at that step covered `kr`, `rrk`,
`rrr`, `Rri`, and `` rri` ``. No dependencies or broader compatibility/performance claims
were added.

## Basic p, ph and f consonants

Added on 2026-10-28: lowercase `p`, `ph`, and `f`.

| Input | Output | Unicode |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `p` | প | U+09AA |
| `ph` | ফ | U+09AB |
| `f` | ফ | U+09AB |

The existing longest-match rule consumes `ph` as one two-key pattern before
single `p`. Both spellings of ফ establish the same consonant context: `phi` and
`fi` → `ফি`, `phrri` and `frri` → `ফৃ`. Other examples include `pani` → `পানি`,
`pakhi` → `পাখি`, and `phOn`/`fOn` → `ফোন`. The core now implements 18 consonant
input sequences producing 16 distinct letters.

The 115 independently authored [fixtures](../tests/fixtures/consonants-p-ph-f.tsv)
were checked against the pinned parser before Rust changes. They cover the three
patterns, all supported vowels/aliases before and after them, digits, separators,
copied Unicode, and words. All 937 reference cases match. Context resets remain
unchanged: `p1a` → `প১আ`, `ph🙂a` → `ফ🙂আ`, and `fকa` → `ফকআ`.

Uppercase aliases, conjuncts, and larger overlapping sequences remain incomplete:

| Input | Current partial engine | Reference |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `pr` | পর | প্র (U+09AA U+09CD U+09B0) |
| `pph` | পফ | প্পহ (U+09AA U+09CD U+09AA U+09B9) |
| `P` | P | প (U+09AA) |
| `F` | F | ফ (U+09AB) |
| `` p`h `` | `` প`হ `` | পহ (U+09AA U+09B9) |

The last example still needs escape handling; the `l`/`h` group below adds
standalone `h`.
Matching `ph` does not establish handling of all sequences containing `p` and `h`.
Other remaining consonants, conjunct/reph forms, punctuation, composition, and
platform integration remain incomplete. Gap probes at that step covered `pr`, `pph`,
`P`, `F`, and `` p`h ``. No dependencies or broader compatibility/performance
claims were added.

## Basic c, ch, j and jh consonants

Added on 2026-10-28: exact lowercase `c`, `ch`, `j`, and `jh`.

| Input | Output | Unicode |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `c` | চ | U+099A |
| `ch` | ছ | U+099B |
| `j` | জ | U+099C |
| `jh` | ঝ | U+099D |

The existing longest-match rule selects `ch` and `jh` before `c` and `j`.
All four establish consonant context for supported vowel signs: `ci` → `চি`,
`chI` → `ছী`, `jOU` → `জৌ`, and `jhrri` → `ঝৃ`. Examples include `cabi` → `চাবি`,
`chabi` → `ছাবি`, `jani` → `জানি`, and `jhuri` → `ঝুরি`. The core now implements
22 consonant input sequences producing 20 distinct letters.

The 148 independently authored [fixtures](../tests/fixtures/consonants-c-ch-j-jh.tsv)
were checked against the pinned parser before Rust changes. They cover all four
patterns alone, with every supported vowel/alias before and after them, digits,
separators, copied Unicode, and words. All 1,085 reference cases match. Existing
boundary behavior remains: `ch1a` → `ছ১আ`, `jh🙂a` → `ঝ🙂আ`, and `jকa` → `জকআ`.

At that step, conjuncts, uppercase variants, and escapes remained incomplete.
The later [remaining conjunct batch](#remaining-exact-conjunct-batch) resolves
`cch`/`jjh` and adds following `r` after `c`/`ch`/`j`/`jh`.

| Input | Engine at that step | Reference |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `cch` | চছ | চ্ছ (U+099A U+09CD U+099B) |
| `jjh` | জঝ | জ্ঝ (U+099C U+09CD U+099D) |
| `C` | C | চ (U+099A) |
| `Jh` | Jহ | জহ (U+099C U+09B9) |
| `` c`h `` | `` চ`হ `` | চহ (U+099A U+09B9) |

Case is significant: reference `Jh` produces জহ, while lowercase `jh` produces
ঝ. No blanket case normalization was added. Other remaining consonants,
conjunct/reph forms, punctuation, composition, and platform integration remain
incomplete. Gap probes at that step covered the five inputs above. No dependencies
or broader compatibility/performance claims were added.

## Basic d, dh, D and Dh consonants

Added on 2026-10-28: exact `d`, `dh`, `D`, and `Dh`.

| Input | Output | Unicode |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `d` | দ | U+09A6 |
| `dh` | ধ | U+09A7 |
| `D` | ড | U+09A1 |
| `Dh` | ঢ | U+09A2 |

The existing longest-match rule selects `dh` before `d` and `Dh` before `D`.
Case distinguishes দ/ধ from ড/ঢ: `da dha Da Dha` → `দা ধা ডা ঢা`.
All four establish consonant context for supported vowel signs, including
`di` → `দি`, `dhI` → `ধী`, `DOI` → `ডৈ`, and `Dhrri` → `ঢৃ`.
The core now implements 26 consonant input sequences producing 24 distinct letters.

The 164 independently authored [fixtures](../tests/fixtures/consonants-d-dh.tsv)
were checked against the pinned parser. They cover all four
patterns alone, every supported vowel/alias before and after them, following-vowel
transitions, digits, boundaries, copied Unicode, case distinctions, and words.
All 1,249 reference cases match. Examples include `din` → `দিন`, `dhan` → `ধান`,
`Dan` → `ডান`, `Dhaka` → `ঢাকা`, and `dOkan` → `দোকান`.
Copied characters still reset context: `dh1a` → `ধ১আ`, `Dh🙂a` → `ঢ🙂আ`,
and `Dকa` → `ডকআ`. Separate calls share no state.

Conjuncts, uppercase-`H` aliases, and escapes remain incomplete:

| Input | Current partial engine | Reference |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `ddh` | দধ | দ্ধ (U+09A6 U+09CD U+09A7) |
| `DDh` | ডঢ | ড্ডহ (U+09A1 U+09CD U+09A1 U+09B9) |
| `dH` | দH | ধ (U+09A7) |
| `DH` | ডH | ঢ (U+09A2) |
| `` d`h `` | `` দ`হ `` | দহ (U+09A6 U+09B9) |

Gap probes at that step covered these five inputs. The later
[d/dh batch](#d-and-dh-conjunct-batch) resolves `ddh` and `DDh`. Uppercase `A` remains unsupported;
use `dhan`/`Dan` in this slice. Other remaining consonants, conjunct/reph forms,
punctuation, composition, and platform integration remain incomplete.
No dependencies or broader compatibility/performance claims were added.

## Basic l and h consonants

Added on 2026-10-28: exact lowercase `l` → ল (U+09B2) and `h` → হ (U+09B9).
Both establish consonant context for supported vowels: `li` → `লি`, `hU` → `হূ`,
`lOI` → `লৈ`, and `hrri` → `হৃ`. The core now implements 28 consonant input
sequences producing 26 distinct letters.

The longest-match rule still selects the existing two-key consonants before
standalone `h`: `kh` → `খ`, while `khh` → `খহ` and `khha` → `খহা`.
The same checks cover `gh`, `th`, `Th`, `bh`, `ph`, `ch`, `jh`, `dh`, and `Dh`.
Space still separates matches: `k h` → `ক হ`, `d h D h` → `দ হ ড হ`.
No conjunct or escape rules were added; a backtick remains literal even though
its following `h` now converts (`` d`h `` → `` দ`হ ``).

The 110 independently authored [fixtures](../tests/fixtures/consonants-l-h.tsv)
were checked against the pinned parser before Rust implementation. They cover
both letters alone, all supported vowels/aliases on either side, following-vowel
transitions, existing two-key consonants followed by `h`/`ha`, digits, boundaries,
copied Unicode, and words. All 1,359 reference cases match. Examples include
`lal` → `লাল`, `holud` → `হলুদ`, `likhi` → `লিখি`, `bhalO` → `ভালো`, and
`DhOl` → `ঢোল`. Copied characters still reset context: `l1a` → `ল১আ`,
`h🙂a` → `হ🙂আ`, and `hকa` → `হকআ`. Separate calls share no state.

Conjuncts, uppercase aliases, and escapes remain incomplete:

| Input | Current partial engine | Reference |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `ll` | লল | ল্ল (U+09B2 U+09CD U+09B2) |
| `hl` | হল | হ্ল (U+09B9 U+09CD U+09B2) |
| `L` | L | ল (U+09B2) |
| `H` | H | হ (U+09B9) |
| `` l`h `` | `` ল`হ `` | লহ (U+09B2 U+09B9) |

Gap probes at that step covered these five inputs. The later
[nasal/h batch](#nasal-and-h-conjunct-batch) resolves `hl`. Remaining consonants, conjunct/reph
forms, other uppercase aliases, punctuation, composition, and platform integration
remain incomplete. No dependencies or broader compatibility/performance claims
were added.

## Confirmed s, sh, S and Sh mapping

Confirmed by the user and implemented on 2026-10-28:

| Input | Output | Unicode |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `s` | স | U+09B8 |
| `sh` | শ | U+09B6 |
| `S` | শ | U+09B6 |
| `Sh` | ষ | U+09B7 |

All four standalone forms match the SHA-256-verified
[pinned reference](#phonetic-behavior-reference), checked with Node.js v24.21.0.
This corrects the earlier planned three-form shorthand: uppercase `S` produces
শ, while `Sh` produces ষ. Case and longest matching matter: `sh` must match
before `s`, and `Sh` before `S`.

All four establish consonant context for supported vowels: `sI` → `সী`,
`shU` → `শূ`, `SOI` → `শৈ`, and `Shrri` → `ষৃ`. `sh` and `S` produce the same
letter, while case distinguishes `Sh` → `ষ`. The core now implements 32 consonant
input sequences producing 29 distinct letters.

The 174 independently authored [fixtures](../tests/fixtures/consonants-s-sh.tsv)
were checked against the pinned parser before Rust implementation. They cover
all supported vowels/aliases on either side, following-vowel transitions,
case distinctions, overlapping sequences, digits, boundaries, copied Unicode,
and words. All 1,533 reference cases match. Examples include `sokal` → `সকাল`,
`shishu`/`SiSu` → `শিশু`, and `bhaSha` → `ভাষা`. The engine follows the raw keys:
`sisu` → `সিসু`, without spelling correction. Longer matching includes `ssh` → `সশ`,
`shSh` → `শষ`, and `Shsh` → `ষশ`; extra `h` remains separate after a two-key
match (`shh` → `শহ`, `Shh` → `ষহ`). Spaces separate keys (`s h S h` → `স হ শ হ`).

Copied characters still reset context: `sh1a` → `শ১আ`, `Sh🙂a` → `ষ🙂আ`,
and `Sকa` → `শকআ`. Separate calls share no state.

At that step, conjuncts, uppercase-`H` aliases, and escapes were incomplete:

| Input | Prior partial engine | Reference |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `sk` | সক | স্ক (U+09B8 U+09CD U+0995) |
| `st` | সত | স্ত (U+09B8 U+09CD U+09A4) |
| `ShT` | ষট | ষ্ট (U+09B7 U+09CD U+099F) |
| `sH` | সH | শ (U+09B6) |
| `` s`h `` | `` স`হ `` | সহ (U+09B8 U+09B9) |

Gap probes at that step covered these five inputs. The consuming k-family batch
resolves `sk`; the [s/Sh stop batch](#s-and-sh-stop-conjunct-batch) resolves
`st`/`ShT`. Remaining consonants, conjunct/reph
forms, other uppercase aliases, punctuation, composition, and platform integration
remain incomplete. No dependencies or broader compatibility/performance claims
were added.

## Basic uppercase R and Rh consonants

Added on 2026-10-28: exact `R` → ড় (U+09DC) and `Rh` → ঢ় (U+09DD).
The existing longest-match rule selects `Rh` before `R`. Both establish consonant
context for supported vowels: `Ri` → `ড়ি`, `RhU` → `ঢ়ূ`, `ROI` → `ড়ৈ`, and
`Rhrri` → `ঢ়ৃ`. The core now implements 34 consonant input sequences producing
31 distinct letters.

Matching remains case-sensitive: `r R Rh` → `র ড় ঢ়`. The lowercase `rri` vowel
still takes priority after either new consonant (`Rrri` → `ড়ৃ`, `RrriR` → `ড়ৃড়`);
this does not add general conjunct handling. An extra `h` after `Rh` remains
separate (`Rhh` → `ঢ়হ`), and a space interrupts matching (`R h` → `ড় হ`).

The 94 independently authored [fixtures](../tests/fixtures/consonants-r-retroflex.tsv)
were checked against the pinned parser before Rust implementation. They cover
all supported vowels/aliases on either side, following-vowel transitions,
lowercase `r`/`rri` interactions, digits, boundaries, copied Unicode, and words.
All 1,627 reference cases match. Examples include `baRi` → `বাড়ি`, `gaRi` → `গাড়ি`,
`gaRh` → `গাঢ়`, and `drriRh` → `দৃঢ়`. Copied characters still reset context:
`R1a` → `ড়১আ`, `Rh🙂a` → `ঢ়🙂আ`, and `Rকa` → `ড়কআ`. Separate calls share no state.

Generated letters use single U+09DC/U+09DD code points, matching the reference.
Copied decomposed letters remain unchanged: the initial ড় in `ড়Ri` → `ড়ড়ি`
keeps U+09A1 U+09BC, followed by generated U+09DC U+09BF. The equivalent fixture
for ঢ় retains U+09A2 U+09BC before U+09DD U+09BF. No Unicode normalization occurs.

Conjuncts, uppercase-`H` aliases, and escapes remain incomplete:

| Input | Engine at that step | Reference |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `Rri` | ড়রি | ড়্রি (U+09DC U+09CD U+09B0 U+09BF) |
| `Rr` | ড়র | ড়্র (U+09DC U+09CD U+09B0) |
| `Rhr` | ঢ়র | ঢ়্র (U+09DD U+09CD U+09B0) |
| `RH` | ড়H | ঢ় (U+09DD) |
| `` R`h `` | `` ড়`হ `` | ড়হ (U+09DC U+09B9) |

Gap probes at that step covered these five inputs. Remaining consonants, conjunct/reph
forms, other uppercase aliases, punctuation, composition, and platform integration
remain incomplete. No dependencies or broader compatibility/performance claims
were added.

The later [q/z/R/Rh following-r batch](#following-r-after-q-z-r-and-rh)
resolves the basic following-r gaps above. Other outputs in the table are
historical; see the current handoff for remaining limitations.

## Basic lowercase z consonant

Added on 2026-10-28: exact lowercase `z` → য (U+09AF), distinct from `j` → জ
(U+099C). It establishes consonant context for supported vowels: `zi` → `যি`,
`zU` → `যূ`, `zOI` → `যৈ`, and `zrri` → `যৃ`. The core now implements
35 consonant input sequences producing 32 distinct letters.

The 51 independently authored [fixtures](../tests/fixtures/consonant-z.tsv)
were checked against the pinned parser before Rust implementation. They cover
all supported vowels/aliases on either side, following-vowel transitions,
repeated and neighboring input, boundaries, digits, copied Unicode, and words.
All 1,678 reference cases match. Examples include `zai` → `যাই`, `zabe` → `যাবে`,
`zodi` → `যদি`, `zokhon` → `যখন`, and `zOg` → `যোগ`. Raw case stays meaningful:
`zini` → `যিনি`, `zIni` → `যীনি`, without spelling correction.

Ordinary consonant adjacency is preserved in the selected cases: `kz` → `কয`,
`zk` → `যক`, and `zz` → `যয`. These are not automatic conjuncts. Copied characters
still reset context: `z1a` → `য১আ`, `z🙂a` → `য🙂আ`, and `zকa` → `যকআ`.
Separate calls share no state.

Uppercase `Z` produces the reference's য-ফলা sequence ্য (U+09CD U+09AF),
which joins onto a preceding consonant. It is not an alias for lowercase `z`.
Uppercase `Z` is now implemented in the [later increment](#uppercase-z-with-az-and-oz-forms).
Other conjuncts and escapes remain incomplete:

| Input | Engine at that step | Reference |

| `zr` | যর | য্র (U+09AF U+09CD U+09B0) |
| `` z`h `` | `` য`হ `` | যহ (U+09AF U+09B9) |

Gap probes at that step covered these two inputs plus the now-resolved `Z`,

resolves the basic following-r gaps above. Other outputs in the table are

## Basic Ng and NG consonants

Added on 2026-10-28: exact `Ng` → ঙ (U+0999) and `NG` → ঞ (U+099E).
Both two-key patterns match before single `N` → ণ, preserving the distinctions
in `n N Ng NG` → `ন ণ ঙ ঞ`. They establish consonant context for vowel signs:
`Ngi` → `ঙি`, `NGU` → `ঞূ`, and `NgOI` → `ঙৈ`. The core now implements
37 consonant input sequences producing 34 distinct letters.

The 88 independently authored [fixtures](../tests/fixtures/consonants-ng-nj.tsv)
were checked against the pinned parser before Rust implementation. They cover
both forms alone, supported vowels/aliases on either side except the following
`rri` exception below, following-vowel transitions, repeated and neighboring
input, case distinctions, digits, boundaries, copied Unicode, and words.
All 1,766 reference cases match. Examples include `aNgul` → `আঙুল`,
`raNga` → `রাঙা`, `aNgur` → `আঙুর`, and raw `baNgla` → `বাঙলা`, without spelling
correction. Separate calls share no state. Digits and copied characters still
reset context: `Ng1a` → `ঙ১আ`, `NG🙂a` → `ঞ🙂আ`, and `Ngকa` → `ঙকআ`.

The initial reference check rejected two prepared expectations: the reference
does not use the ৃ sign in `Ngrri` or `NGrri`. These cases were excluded from
that increment's compatibility fixtures and recorded as gaps. They are now
covered by the [following-`rri` increment](#following-rri-after-ng-ng-and-ng) below.

At that step, conjuncts and escapes remained incomplete. Lowercase `ng` is
handled in the next section, and the later [exact `Ngk` increment](#exact-ngk-conjunct)
resolves the `Ngk` case below. The [remaining conjunct batch](#remaining-exact-conjunct-batch)
also resolves `NGc`/`NGch`.

| Input | Engine at that step | Reference |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `Ngk` | ঙক | ঙ্ক (U+0999 U+09CD U+0995) |
| `NGc` | ঞচ | ঞ্চ (U+099E U+09CD U+099A) |
| `NGch` | ঞছ | ঞ্ছ (U+099E U+09CD U+099B) |
| `` N`g `` | `` ণ`গ `` | ণগ (U+09A3 U+0997) |

Gap probes at that step covered lowercase `ng`, `Ngrri`, `NGrri`, and the four
inputs above. The first three are now resolved by the later increments.
Remaining consonants, conjunct/reph
forms, other aliases, punctuation, composition, and platform integration remain
